f           ~
            •
                      from beneath is provided by the heavy rim.

~-          ~
                         The .metal model sailed through the air bcautifullr-
                      but also, bcinll metal, it sailed through windows and

t           ~

            i'
            :;
                      hedg.s and other obstacles, somewhat after the manner of
                      a miniature cyclone. A material other than metal seemed
                      a wise idea if the disc-or flying saucer-were to become

t•          '!
            ;
                      a commercial product.       .
                         A lightweight, slightly flexible plastics looked like the
                      answer, and Morrison brought his metal model to Southern

            .
            ~
                      California Plastic Co., Glendale, Calif., to sec what might
                      be done in plastics. Stanley J. Gray and Edward L Ken-
                      nedy, president and secretary of the firm, took tbe metal
            •
            !         disc out on the lawn to "demonstrate" its possibilities
                      and were soon breathlessly convinced that a great dc:al
     '.:: J.          of fun could be had by young and old alike.
                         A plastics model of the disc was carved from a solid
                      block of material, and then a mold m>de in the shop of
                      Southern California. Engraving of the mold was done
                      by United Engravers, Los Angeles.
                         Then came the problem of the right material. A search
                      was made throughout the rany;e of thermoplastic molding
                      compounds to find the proper material that comhincd
                      fteaibifity, impact strenph, and stability while in motion.
                       (llx' discs achieve considerable speed --and strain--whco
                      in flight.) Cellulose acetate provided the hc<t combination
                      of properties to do the joh. Acetate discs will not break
                      glass windows, nor shatter when falling from a great
                      height, nor scar the finish on automobiles, nor injure the
                      players.

                                                                   RE:       FLYING DISCS

                    Your attention is directed to Bureau Bulletin /r'57, Series 1947, dated
         October 1 1 19471 relating to the discontinuance of investigation b7 this Bureau in
         matters concerning flying discs.

                            For 7our confidential information, a reliable and confidential source
         bas advised the Bureau that f'l7ing discs are believed to be man-made missiles rather
         than natural phenomenon. It bas also been detennined that for approximately the
         past four years the USSR bas been engaged in experimentation on an unknown t7Pe
         of flying disc.

                    !!he Department of the Air Force bas furnished to the Bureau the attached
                                                                                                                                                                                  7    -
         memorandum classified "restricted" dated February 15 1 19491 entitled "l.lllconventional
         Aircrai't." 'lhis memorandum ill being furnished to 70u in order that all agents
         assigned to your office can be infonned of the t7Pe ot information desired by the
         Atr Force in this matter.

                     As set forth in Bureau Bulletin #571 referred to above, no investigation
         should be conducted by your o1'f'1ee ~-ela.t:tve to tl;;..,...ng discs, b.O'ofever, the attached
         memorandUI!I should be referred to in securing data from persons who desire to
         voluntaril7 turnish information to your ottice relating to f'lpng discc.

                                                                                     Veey truly yours 1

              PURPOSE

                        The approval of the EXecutivesConference is requested for the
              attached SAC letter furnishing to the field the type of infor.nation
              desired by the Intelligence Division of the A;i.r Force in connection with
              the captioned matter.

              BACKGROID!D

                          You will recall that by Bureau ~lletin 1151, Series 19h7, dated
              october 1, 1947, the field was advised that effective as of that date the
              Bureau had discontinued its investigative activities in connection with
              flying discs. The field was advised that all future reports concerning
              tl.i.s matter received in t.he field should be referred to the A;i.r Forces.

             it - s learned that some type of flying discs were being experimented

             most all of the flying discs seen by persons in the United States approached
             tnis count.ry from a northerly direction ano returned in the same direct.ion,
             inaicating the strong possibility that they are co~g from Russia.
